   392	        let message = format!("cumulative offset {offset:+}ms (authoritative floor included); process offset lasts until restart{isolated}");
   393	        Ok((now, offset, message, before, boundaries))
   394	    })();
   395	    let (now, offset, message, before, boundaries) = match advanced {
   396	        Ok(result) => result,
   397	        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
   398	    };
   399	    // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — a developer is awaiting this
   400	    // request; synchronous clock-advance work intentionally keeps request QoS.
   401	    let drained = (|| {
   402	        let mut crossed = Vec::new();
   403	        if each_boundary {
   404	            for (index, at) in boundaries {
   405	                crossed.extend(engine_work(shared, |engine, _| engine.fire_schedule_boundary_at(now, index, at))?);
   406	            }
   407	        }
   408	        let mut work = work(shared, true, 0)?;
   409	        work.schedules += crossed.len();
   410	        work.fired.extend(crossed);
   411	        Ok::<_, RuntimeError>(work)
   412	    })();
   413	    match drained.map_err(|error| error.refusal()).and_then(|work| completed_drain(work, crate::lifecycle::stopping())) {
   414	        Ok(work) => {
   415	            let schedules = crate::dev_clock_store::schedule_report(&lock(&shared.engine), &before, &work.fired, now, shared.hold_schedules, each_boundary);
   416	            respond(request, 200, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "skewMs":offset, "eachBoundary":each_boundary, "message":message, "work":work, "schedules":schedules}))
   417	        }
   418	        // Time remains advanced if maintenance fails; callers must not
   419	        // retry the advance blindly. The normal tick retries pending work.
   420	        Err(error) => respond(request, 500, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "skewMs":offset, "eachBoundary":each_boundary, "message":message, "denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
   421	    }
   422	}
   423	
   424	/// `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` with `{"args":{..}}`: queue one run of a job as the
   425	/// system on the dev clock and drive chunks until it is done or parked. This is
   426	/// what `snapback4 run <job>` forwards to while dev owns the store.
   427	pub(super) fn run_job(request: Request, shared: &Shared, name: &str, body: &str) {
   428	    use snapback4_core::{JobState, error::Refusal, ir::ProgramKind, wire};
   429	    let service_route=request.url().starts_with("/jobs/");
   430	    let service=if service_route {
   431	        match super::session_identity::principal_of(&request,shared) {
   432	            Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind=="service" && principal.starts_with("service:") => Some(principal),
   433	            _ => return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","triggering a deployed system job requires a full service token").guide("jobs"))})),
   434	        }
   435	    } else {
   436	        if !shared.personas {return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","/dev/jobs is a development door; use run <job> --release <dir> with a service token").guide("jobs"))}));}
   437	        None
   438	    };
   439	    let refuse = |request, status, refusal: Refusal| respond(request, status, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&refusal.guide("jobs"))}));
   440	    let payload: serde_json::Value = match serde_json::from_str(if body.trim().is_empty() { "{}" } else { body }) {
   441	        Ok(payload) => payload,
   442	        Err(error) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("the request body is not JSON: {error}; send {{\"args\":{{..}}}}"))),
   443	    };
   444	    if service_route && !shared.personas {crate::release_requests::Scope::admit(&shared.release_requests,||*lock(&shared.generation));}
   445	    let program = lock(&shared.bound).programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == name).map(|p| p.program.clone());
   446	    let program = match program {
   447	        Some(program) if program.kind == ProgramKind::Job => program,
   448	        Some(other) => return refuse(request, 404, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("{name} is a {}, not a job; queries are POST /q/<op>, mutations POST /m/<op>", other.kind.word()))),
   449	        None => return respond(request, 404, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&super::dev_doors::stale(shared, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("unknown job {name}")).guide("jobs")))})),
   450	    };
   451	    if let Some(raw)=payload.get("args") && let Some(why)=super::dev_doors::persona_argument(shared,&program.args,raw) {return refuse(request,400,why);}
   452	    let mut args = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
   453	    if let Some(fields) = payload.get("args").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_object) {
   454	        for (field, value) in fields {
   455	            let Some(kind) = program.args.get(field) else { return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("{name} takes no argument {field}"))); };
   456	            match wire::from_json(kind, value) {
   457	                Ok(typed) => { args.insert(field.clone(), typed); }
   458	                Err(why) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("argument {field}: {why}"))),
   459	            }
   460	        }
   461	    }
   462	    let mut admitted=lock(&shared.engine);
   463	    crate::release_requests::stamp(admitted.generation());
   464	    if !service_route && let Err(why) = shared.dev_authority(&admitted) { drop(admitted); return refuse(request,403,why); }
   465	    if let Some(service)=&service {
   466	        if let Err(why)=super::session_identity::mutation_authority(&request,shared,&admitted,service) {drop(admitted);return refuse(request,403,why);}
   467	    }
   468	    let queued=admitted.enqueue_job(name,args,shared.now());
   469	    drop(admitted);
   470	    crate::release_requests::executed();
   471	    let id = match queued {
   472	        Ok(id) => id,
   473	        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
   474	    };
   475	    // FIFO: earlier queued runs finish first, as with `run` on a stopped store;
   476	    // the background tick may run some chunks too, through the same engine.
   477	    let run = loop {
   478	        let current = {let engine=lock(&shared.engine);crate::release_requests::stamp(engine.generation());engine.job(&id)};
   479	        match current {
   480	            Ok(Some(run)) if matches!(run.state, JobState::Done | JobState::Parked) || crate::lifecycle::stopping() => break run,
   481	            Ok(Some(_)) => {}
   482	            Ok(None) => return respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&RuntimeError::Store(format!("job {id} vanished")).refusal())})),
   483	            Err(error) => return respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
   484	        }
   485	        let (chunk, logs) = engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| (engine.run_job_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget), engine.take_native_logs()));
   486	        for line in logs { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: native {line}"); }
   487	        match chunk {
   488	            Ok(Some(chunk)) if chunk.state == JobState::Parked && shared.personas => shared.refusals.job(&chunk),
   489	            Ok(_) => {}
   490	            Err(error) => return respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
     1	//! Development doors beside the operation routes: `POST /seed`, `POST /dev/reset`, and the
     2	//! `--no-watch` hint that names a schema edited on disk after adoption.
     3	use super::{Shared, lock, refusal_json, respond_timed};
     4	use crate::project::{Project, SourceStamp};
     5	use serde_json::{Value as Json, json};
     6	use snapback4_core::error::Refusal;
     7	use snapback4_core::wire;
     8	use std::collections::BTreeMap;
     9	use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
    10	use std::sync::Mutex;
    11	use tiny_http::Request;
    12	
    13	/// The source stamp each `--no-watch` dev host adopted, by project root.
    14	static PINNED: Mutex<BTreeMap<PathBuf, SourceStamp>> = Mutex::new(BTreeMap::new());
    15	
    16	pub(super) fn closed() -> Refusal {
    17	    Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "development doors are closed; use serve staging with real users (guide staging), or explicitly opt into dev --unsafe-dev-doors for local fixtures").guide("staging")
    18	}
    19	
    20	pub(super) fn pin(project: &Path, stamp: SourceStamp) {
    21	    PINNED.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).insert(project.to_owned(), stamp);
    22	}
    23	
    24	/// A `--no-watch` host serves the schema it started with. When a request
    25	/// names an operation or argument that host does not know and the `.q` files
    26	/// changed on disk since, say so: the edit is real, it was just never adopted.
    27	pub(super) fn stale(shared: &Shared, refusal: Refusal) -> Refusal {
    28	    let Some(adopted) = PINNED.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).get(&shared.project).copied() else { return refusal };
    29	    if (Project { root: shared.project.clone() }).sources_stamp() == adopted { return refusal; }
    30	    let message = format!("{}; this dev host runs with --no-watch and snapback/ changed on disk after it adopted its schema, so the edit is not served", refusal.message);
    31	    Refusal { message, rewrite: Some("restart `snapback4 dev` (or drop --no-watch so saves re-adopt), then retry".into()), ..refusal }
    32	}
    33	
    34	// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — persona names are not their principals; dev only.
    35	pub(super) fn persona_argument(shared: &Shared, kinds: &BTreeMap<String,snapback4_core::ir::ArgType>, args: &Json) -> Option<Refusal> {
    36	    if !shared.personas { return None; }
    37	    let bound = lock(&shared.bound);
    38	    crate::persona_principals::arguments(&bound.personas,kinds,args)
    39	}
    40	// Rows are written as the system principal; each names its id because seed is an upsert by id.
    41	pub(super) fn seed(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
    42	    if !shared.dev_doors() || !request.remote_addr().is_some_and(|a| a.ip().is_loopback()) {
    43	        respond_timed(request, shared, 403, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "seeding is a development door")) }));
    44	        return;
    45	    }
    46	    let refuse = |request, refusal: Refusal| respond_timed(request, shared, 400, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&refusal.guide("personas")) }));
    47	    let rows: Vec<Json> = match serde_json::from_str(body) {
    48	        Ok(Json::Array(rows)) => rows,
    49	        _ => return refuse(request, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "seed takes a JSON array of {table, row}")),
    50	    };
    51	    let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
    52	    if let Err(why) = shared.dev_authority(&engine) { drop(engine); return respond_timed(request, shared, 403, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&why)})); }
    53	    let now = shared.now();
    54	    let typed = match typed_rows(engine.schema(), &rows, now) {
    55	        Ok(typed) => typed,
    56	        Err(refusal) => { drop(engine); return refuse(request, refusal); }
    57	    };
    58	    let outcome = engine.seed_at(typed, now);
    59	    drop(engine);
    60	    match outcome {
    61	        Ok(count) => {
    62	            shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine));
    63	            respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "seeded": count }));
    64	        }
    65	        Err(error) => respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&seed_refusal(error)) })),
    66	    }
    67	}
    68	
    69	/// `/seed` rows checked against the served schema and typed, or the first problem.
    70	fn typed_rows(schema: &snapback4_core::Schema, rows: &[Json], now: i64) -> Result<Vec<(String, snapback4_core::Value)>, Refusal> {
    71	    let mut typed = Vec::new();
    72	    for (index, entry) in rows.iter().enumerate() {
    73	        let (Some(table), Some(row)) = (entry.get("table").and_then(Json::as_str), entry.get("row")) else {
    74	            return Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "each seed entry is {table, row}"));
    75	        };
    76	        let Some(definition) = schema.table(table) else {
    77	            return Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("unknown table {table}")));
    78	        };
    79	        if row.is_object() && row.get("id").is_none_or(|id| !id.is_string()) {
    80	            return Err(missing_id(index, table));
    81	        }
    82	        let mut row = row.clone();
    83	        crate::verbs::test_fixture::relative_times(definition, &mut row, now)
    84	            .map_err(|why| Refusal::new("E_FIXTURE_TIME", "input", format!("seed row {index} ({table}): {why}")))?;
    85	        if let Some(why) = wire::bare_principal(definition, &row) {
    86	            return Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("seed row {index} ({table}): {why}")));
    87	        }
    88	        match wire::row_from_json(definition, &row) {
    89	            Ok(value) => typed.push((table.to_owned(), value)),
    90	            Err(why) => return Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("{table}: {why}"))),
   125	            }
   126	            Ok(request)
   127	        }
   128	        _ => Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", SHAPE)),
   129	    }
   130	}
   131	
   132	// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.25 — one transaction: empty, new identity, reseed; then the host's caches follow.
   133	pub(super) fn reset(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
   134	    if !shared.dev_doors() || !request.remote_addr().is_some_and(|a| a.ip().is_loopback()) {
   135	        respond_timed(request, shared, 403, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&reset_release()) }));
   136	        return;
   137	    }
   138	    let started = std::time::Instant::now();
   139	    let refuse = |request, refusal: Refusal| respond_timed(request, shared, 400, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&refusal.guide("testing")) }));
   140	    let asked = match reset_request(body) {
   141	        Ok(asked) => asked,
   142	        Err(refusal) => return refuse(request, refusal),
   143	    };
   144	    // Serialize with clock advances, then take the engine before the clock:
   145	    // the order clock advance and worker claims use.
   146	    let advance = lock(&shared.maintenance).advance.clone();
   147	    let _advance = lock(&advance);
   148	    let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
   149	    if let Err(why) = shared.dev_authority(&engine) { drop(engine); return respond_timed(request, shared, 403, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&why)})); }
   150	    let mut clock = lock(&shared.clock).clone();
   151	    if asked.clock && let Err(refusal) = clock.reset() { drop(engine); return refuse(request, refusal); }
   152	    let now = clock.now();
   153	    // Resolve against the prospective reset clock before changing any durable state.
   154	    let typed = match typed_rows(engine.schema(), &asked.seed, now) {
   155	        Ok(typed) => typed,
   156	        Err(refusal) => { drop(engine); return refuse(request, refusal); }
   157	    };
   158	    let reset = match engine.dev_reset_at(typed, now) {
   159	        Ok(reset) => reset,
   160	        Err(error) => {
   161	            drop(engine);
   162	            return respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&seed_refusal(error)) }));
   163	        }
   164	    };
   165	    if asked.clock { *lock(&shared.clock) = clock; }
   166	    // The reset committed a new identity and a new authority clock. Replace,
   167	    // rather than max with, the retired store's cached high-water mark.
   168	    shared.time_audience_clock.store(if engine.schema().tables.values().any(|t| t.not_before.is_some()) { now } else { i64::MIN }, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Release);
   169	    // Detached readers reopen; the following registry, change answers and
   170	    // `/dev/status` forget the old identity. Wake then names the new store,
   171	    // so every held poll returns and each client sees the replacement.
   172	    let sites = lock(&shared.bound).refusal_sites.clone();
   173	    lock(&shared.readers).replace(&engine, &sites);
   174	    lock(&shared.adoption).store_id = reset.store_id.clone();
   175	    if let Ok(reader) = snapback4_core::engine::following_history::Reader::new() { *lock(&shared.following) = reader; }
   176	    *lock(&shared.change_answers) = Default::default();
   177	    shared.durable_committed(&engine);
   178	    let generation = engine.generation();
   179	    drop(engine);
   180	    let assets = reset.assets.len();
   181	    if let Err(error) = reset.assets.remove_files() { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: reset left asset bytes behind: {error}"); }
   182	    let now = shared.now();
   183	    snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: store reset (store {} → {}, seeded {}) in {} ms", reset.previous_store_id, reset.store_id, reset.seeded, started.elapsed().as_millis());
   184	    respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({
   185	        "reset": true, "seeded": reset.seeded, "generation": generation, "storeId": reset.store_id,

### 3.4 P8: residency you can see, and a CLI that does not fight the dev server

**Change.**

- `client.residency()` returns rows and bytes per table on the device, with
  the store's total bytes and, on the web, the wasm heap. The guide's residency
  card shows it next to the sync windows that bound it.
- `snapback4 run <job>` and the other store-writing CLI commands detect a live
  dev server on the same store (its lifetime lock file) and either run the work
  through that server or refuse with `E_STORE_BUSY`, naming the server's
  address, instead of failing with `database is locked`. Which of the two is
  the lane's first finding.

**Evidence.** Residency on a fixture with known rows; the CLI against a running
dev server, both ways.

**2026-10-08 — `run` goes through a live dev host (app-farm round 1).** The
lane's first finding was the refusal; 27 of ~270 app-farm builds (41 diaries)
then stopped their dev server to run one operation, or reverse-engineered the
wire. `run` now takes the other branch of this item when the owner is a
responsive `snapback4 dev` (it answers `GET /dev/status`): a query or mutation
goes to `POST /q|/m/<op>` with the persona header naming the same principal a
local run would use, and a job to the dev-only `POST /dev/jobs/<job>`, which
queues it behind earlier runs and drives chunks on the dev clock. No second
writer opens the store. `--now`, a non-persona principal, `serve` and a stopped
host still refuse `E_STORE_BUSY`, now with the equivalent `curl`. Other
store-writing commands keep the refusal.

**2026-09-27 — Darwin ownership correction (lane OW).** Ownership now locks
`<database filename>-owner` beside the canonical database, a regular 0600 file
opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`: exclusive for backend writers, shared for devices
including the native C ABI. On macOS/iOS, a flock on the database itself
conflicts with SQLite's POSIX locks, preventing startup adoption and device
commits. Database identity remains `(dev, ino)`, checked with stat alone;
only a missing database is created and closed before SQLite opens it. The
guard holds the sidecar, never a database descriptor whose close could discard
SQLite's locks. Sidecars persist after unlock and move with the database's
other sidecars; symlinks, hard links and nonregular ownership files are refused.
`serve.lock` remains an address hint, and `E_STORE_BUSY` keeps its meaning.

**2026-09-27 — round-1 file-mutation hardening (lane OW).** Both ownership
and address-file opens lstat the database and candidate before opening and
compare the opened inode again before any lock, read, write or chmod. A
post-open alias or uncertain descriptor is deliberately forgotten, never
closed or unlocked: either operation could discard SQLite's POSIX locks.
Ownership sidecars must be empty, and address records at most 128 bytes;
these stat-only bounds also refuse a SQLite database renamed to either name
when its original path is absent. Lock the sidecar before creating a missing
database; an `E_STORE_BUSY` probe creates no database or address record.
Database hard links are refused with `E_STORE` and “copy the store, do not
hard-link it,” including on an existing owner's path check.

**2026-09-27 — round-2 exclusion boundary (lane OW, option b).** Exclusion
### 3.6 P10: adding a view never stalls reads

**AP follow-up, 2026-10-04.** Calibration 30 exposed the remaining writer cost
on a different real schema delta: nine indexes, three historical captures and
seven public views. Server activation orders admitted index keys before insertion,
uses a configurable combined main/temp cache target (default 64 MiB, half each),
and reads ahead at most 32 serialized
rows / 128 KiB for its single-row maintained-count scans. The latter keeps every
per-row meter charge and recount boundary; device execution is unchanged.
Count assessment shares one source validation when a whole-source row count
proves every group fits, while sums retain wide accumulation. Sparse projection
and page admission avoid unnecessary copies/decodes. These are changes inside
the same transaction, not online adoption. `N_ADOPTION_PAUSE` exposes source
counts and modeled “up to” work at measured eb1000 rates before the lock; the
subtotal is not a floor and neither number is a latency bound. Retirement-only
and mixed changes count removed/rebuilt predecessor rows and index entries once
per catalog name and explicitly warn of the additional unestimated pause.
Round 2 includes surviving timeline-owned names, dependent aggregate targets,
row-view recount/basis rows and replaced indexes. Tables selected by
`Retirement::affects` (read-through changes or inherited-expiry field/default
changes) warn about retirement/recapture: the deletion subset depends on
activation time, so their stored populations are not exact deletion counts.
On a schema change, an unaffected read-through table separately rewrites every
stored row, including expired rows, to recompute deadlines. These deadline
rewrites are counted exactly; they are not a deletion subset. Their pause
remains unestimated. Maintained-column mirror passes, cap occupancy work and
asset-home ledger retirement also name unestimated work. These populations can
overlap modeled source visits. Legacy live-sum and cap-occupancy repairs remain
visible with no schema delta; preview performs read-only counts, never retirement or row rewrites. Advice also names `SNAPBACK4_ADOPTION_CACHE_MIB`
(0 preserves the caller setting) and the approximately 147 MiB RSS measured with
the round-0 default; cache size is not a process cap. Linux reduces the target
for finite address-space headroom. A borrowed-cache SQLite
`NOMEM` rolls back fully and retries once at the caller setting; publication is
still one atomic commit. Completed adoption prints disjoint phases with nested
index/capture/view attribution, including failed-attempt time when retried. The
[AP report](../snapback4/reports/20261004-ap-adoption-pause/REPORT.md) carries the
new before/after and equivalence evidence; the historical measurements below
remain measurements of their original fixtures.

Calibration #22's M8 read waited 74,978 ms while adoption held the serving
engine. A reproducible engine fixture at the reported source populations has
1,447,960 rows and 8,863,718 index entries. Before optimization, on the lane's
Linux host, test-profile adoption took 171,946 ms and its waiting read took
171,948 ms. Unrelated row projection and index replacement took 142,046 ms;
validation 12,850 ms; commit/checkpoint 6,776 ms. The six new grouped folds
already group in memory and write one row per final key: together 671 ms.
Reference capture took another 2,157 ms. Source index changes also selected a
3,612 ms rebuild of the unchanged unread view. Full evidence and reproduction
are in [the lane report](../snapback4/reports/20260926-lane-ad-adoption.md).

**Correction and final direction.** The first fixture removed every operation
after the six views, including unrelated trending/digest queries. Its profile
correctly identifies the whole-store work but its unread rebuild is not part of
the requested M7→M8 delta. The corrected M7 retains those operations and omits
# LLP 3000.000.000: Retained storage and hot release activation

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Schema evolution, Storage, CLI, Deployment, Replica generations
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (Workstream ROLL)
**Date:** 2026-10-12
**Revised:** 2026-10-16 (PREV wave-13 preview decisions §13); 2026-10-15 (SURF readiness/closeout; aggregate identity migration covers retained references; PUBFIX28: consistent disjoint preview prose and blocking accepted control streams); 2026-10-14 (NARR wave-11 amendment); 2026-10-13 (§10: REL2 wave-10 release lifecycle); 2026-10-13 (MACFIX: explicit pre-retention compatibility boundary and canonical deployment/worker paths); 2026-10-12 (§5.1: path-independent control socket; implemented; dual-family confirmation, captured lifetimes, native generations, hot activation and complete qualification)
**Related:** [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md), [LLP 3000.000](./3000.000-atomic-schema-and-release-activation.research.md), [LLP 3000.007](./3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.019](./3000.019-what-the-builder-still-wrote.rfc.md)

Staging store classification and real-user provisioning: [LLP 3000.000.000.001](./3000.000.000.001-staging-with-real-users.rfc.md) (STAGE).

## 1. Commission and scope

Charlie approved the ideal end-game release lifecycle and commissioned implementation on 2026-10-12. Implementer: this ROLL session, beginning that date. Wave 8 DT1 found additive rollback deletes new storage in all 50 day-two lanes; DT1a found unnamed enum conflicts; DT10 found deployment requires stopping serve. This supersedes the release-only removal semantics in LLP 3000.000's implementation follow-up. Development and device replacement keep their existing projection/reset semantics. No new publication authority or gate is introduced.

## 2. Retain by default

Every release activation (deploy and rollback) retains removed durable application tables, columns and index definitions in SQLite-owned dormant storage within the same activation transaction. The active r/i image remains an exact projection of the active schema. Dormant data is outside that namespace and unavailable to programs, rules, references, queries, sync, SQL application projections and clients. The Quarry/IR binder admits only declared application table names; it has no general application SQL evaluator. Dormant internal names are not added to Schema, and schema validation forbids declarations in the reserved `_dormant_` and `_release_` namespaces. Inspection `snapback4 sql` is owner-only and may enumerate it. A future application SQL adapter must keep its declared authorized input allow-list. Asset reads must resolve the home table, column and row in the active schema/r image and check the current read rule; a dormant home refuses E_ASSET_UNKNOWN even when a caller knows its id. Test the reserved-name/program boundary and direct fetch by id. Reintroducing a dormant name deliberately reactivates its data, after full validation under the candidate schema. A deployment that reuses any dormant name refuses unless --restore-dormant explicitly selects restoration; its refusal lists retained items/counts/samples and the alternatives `deploy <dir> --restore-dormant` or `prune <dir> --allow-destructive` before fresh creation. Rollback's selection of an immutable older release explicitly selects restoration; `rollback <dir> --to N` also selects an immutable release for deliberate roll forward. To convert durable to ephemeral, deploy a release without that table, prune it explicitly, then deploy the ephemeral definition. This applies to deployments as well as rollback; a rename remains two names, without an inferred data move.

Inventory and audit entries carry a stable typed key (`table:name`, `column:table.name`, or `index:table.name`); pagination uses the returned key, not a bare path that could collide between columns and indexes. The SQLite format is `_dormant_tables(table_name, definition)` for whole-table Table definitions, `_dormant_rows(t,id,data)` for their stored images, `_dormant_columns(table_name,column_name,definition)` for retired column type/default/ownership, `_dormant_values(t,id,column_name,data)` for their cells, `_dormant_indexes(table_name,index_name,definition)` for index definitions and `_dormant_refs(t,id,column_name,parent_t,parent_id,invalid)` for deletion evidence with a parent lookup index. Catalog numbers are reserved monotonically and reused for the same physical name, but dormant rows are never kept in r under a hidden catalog number. These tables are created only during successful release activation; legacy deployments start empty and cannot recover data previously deleted by older binaries. A successful release activation writes the release-only format marker `version=snapback4-release-store-3`. New Store open/version checks accept this, the prior snapback4-release-store-2 and the legacy snapback4-store-1; old binaries reject it with E_STORE rather than opening it. Development and device stores retain snapback4-store-1 and their formats are unchanged. The format marker rolls back with failed activation. On restore, omitted cells from whole-table images move to dormant column cells before projection, merging previous retired cells; full-table entries are consumed only on commit.

The compatibility boundary is the store format, independently of schema
capabilities. Published 0.2.33 refuses retained release-store versions 2 and 3
with `E_STORE: store version <version> is not snapback4-store-1; this data
directory belongs to another Snapback` (the read-only doctor form omits
the final data-directory explanation), including capability-free releases.
That refusal must preserve committed state and never interpret dormant data
under the old layout. Capability-free backend/schema bytes and unchanged
store-1 development/device fixtures retain their separate compatibility
controls (LLP 3000.022 §14.3); they do not establish old-server readability of
new release stores. The Darwin publication fence checks both boundaries.
The MACFIX compatibility/path amendment passed independent final
[Muse](./reviews/3000.000.000-macfix-r3.muse.md) and
[Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000-macfix-r3.codex.md) reviews at e9150bd171.
The required Darwin schema and worker files passed alone with default TMPDIR
(10 and 82 tests); whole-publication qualification is a separate claim.

Dormant whole tables store their row images; dormant columns store values keyed by table/column/row id and their definitions. Capture interpreted literal defaults and optional nulls when a column becomes dormant, preserving the value existing rows actually saw. Keep index definitions as history; preserve their data logically and rebuild physical keys on reactivation from current rows. Dormant indexes are never consulted for active writes, uniqueness or caps. Thus a new duplicate under the older release can precisely refuse forward activation. Derived rows are caches and are rebuilt, not authoritative history. Retention must precede existing derived-state retirement. An unsupported durable-to-ephemeral conversion refuses rather than silently discarding stored data.

Partial restoration consumes only candidate-visible cells: B's T(a,b) → absent T → C's T(a) restores a and moves b to dormant cells, with b's definition, then B restores b. For an identical (t,id,column) cell key, the newly retired active value wins: it is the most recent authoritative value. Preserve other dormant keys. Use transactional upsert and test retire → restore → update → retire → restore. Keep zero-row table/column/index definitions too. Whole-table restoration first restores its stored image, splits omitted cells into dormancy, then projects the active image. Consumption shares commit with validation. Refuse application-table to derived-table ownership changes, or authoritative-column to maintained/captured ownership changes, including when the application table/column was dormant. Derived caches never overwrite retained authoritative data. The refusal names the incompatible owner and directs a deliberate rename or prune while dormant.

At retirement record reference edges for dormant rows/cells (including optional/list references), bound to their current parent lifetime. An active delete transaction marks matching dormant edges invalid before the id can be reused, even for identical replacement values. Only a move into dormant storage or replacement of a derived cache is exempt from deletion invalidation. Actual authoritative-row deletion always invalidates matching dormant edges, including expiry during activation; failed activation rolls the invalidation back. A fixed-size identity migration rewrites dormant whole rows, cells, narrowed rows and their edge ledger when the referenced derived target lifetime survives migration; removing that target retires its lifetime, so its dormant edges remain bound to the retired address and restoration refuses rather than attaching them to a later reintroduction. Restoration refuses invalidated edges or missing parents with table/column/parent ids, counts and samples; it cannot silently recapture another parent's audience. For read-through rows, compare preserved captured audience and identity fields to the candidate parent before existing recapture work. A changed audience refuses restoration; inherited deadlines keep their original capture. Newly expired rows remain logically invisible and may be swept only under the reactivated schema. Test deletion/reuse, identical replacement, changed audience, nested references and expiry. This adds no device incarnation format.

Restoration, candidate validation, derived rebuild, catalog/generation/programs and current release commit together. Any error rolls all of them back. Retained values overwrite no explicit candidate writes: restoration precedes activation builders. Incompatible name reuse must pass candidate types and constraints; no flag bypasses validation.

## 3. Older writes and incompatible values

Updating an active row while its newer columns are dormant preserves those dormant values. Deleting that row deletes its dormant column values in the same transaction: forward never resurrects a deleted row, including when its id is reused. New rows written under the older schema have no dormant value. On forward they receive the candidate literal default or optional null; required columns without such a default refuse with exact missing-row counts and up to five ids. Repair by deploying a bridge schema with that column optional or a literal default using --restore-dormant, fill it through a bridge mutation, then deploy the desired required schema. Alternatively explicitly delete the named rows through the current release and retry. Never tell the older schema to write a field it lacks. Contextual now/viewer defaults do not invent historical values. Existing required-column backfill rules still apply.

Release activations retain incompatible existing values per row rather than refusing a type narrowing. Section 9 specifies the visibility, reference and restoration rules. Development adoption keeps its checked narrowing behavior. Missing newly required fields still need the bridge described above; retention applies to present historical values, not absent backfill. No implicit coercion or fallback is introduced.

For every data-dependent refusal, aggregate count and bounded samples (five distinct values/keys, 256 UTF-8 bytes each, plus truncation markers) at the failed column/index/constraint, without unbounded row retention. Include actual conflicting values for column failures, index name and conflicting keys for unique failures, and actionable command or schema repair. Detailed values are privileged operator diagnostics only, never ordinary application refusal payloads, query meter diagnostics or sync refusals. No claim that a green read-only preview freezes later writes: adoption rechecks under its transaction.

## 4. Explicit pruning and audit

snapback4 prune <dir> runs through the serving owner when live, otherwise the offline owner. It lists dormant tables, columns and indexes with row counts, logical serialized dormant bytes (including retained asset bytes separately) and at most five ids; it changes nothing without --allow-destructive. With that flag it permanently deletes exactly the currently dormant storage in one writer transaction, records a durable prune event, and leaves active rows/programs untouched. It releases dormant asset homes for the ordinary collector. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive do not prune; direct users to the separate command. Prune intentionally deletes all dormant items as one reviewed storage boundary; per-item dependency-aware pruning is deferred because partial pruning can strand dormant references and asset homes. There is no implicit size or age collector: previews make storage growth visible without inventing a data-loss policy. After prune, reintroducing the schema gets empty tables and normal required-column/default behavior. Pruning is an irreversible operator action; the command output names every item dropped.

Release activation events live in the database transaction with activation revision, operation, release/previous/generation, timestamp and retained/restored summary. Prune records its item summary in that transaction. Existing releases.jsonl and current remain checked convenience copies; post-commit filesystem errors explicitly say activation succeeded. History is never edited or rewound. Rollback picks an earlier immutable source, not a historical data snapshot.

Dormant bytes have no automatic age-based deletion: retention lasts until explicit prune, and prune preview exposes populations so operators control disk use. Dormant asset homes remain live in the asset ledger but are unreadable because the active schema has no home. Active-row deletion also retires any associated dormant asset home. Dormant rows do not run old expiry or jobs while dormant; candidate liveness applies again upon reactivation. Explicit application deletion and ordinary active expiry still mean deletion, not an undo archive.

## 5. Hot activation and ownership

serve owns the database sidecar for its lifetime and publishes a Unix-domain release-control socket in the private per-user runtime directory (§5.1; directory 0700, socket 0600). Check peer credentials on both ends (Linux SO_PEERCRED, Darwin getpeereid), requiring the effective UID. Only the host filesystem owner has this capability; it is not an HTTP door, browser origin, bearer service seat or remote endpoint. Check filesystem objects before replacing a stale socket; never follow symlinks or replace a regular file. Use `.release-command` (a hardened private regular file, no-follow, single link) for the command flock and the deterministic runtime address (§5.1) for the control socket. lstat socket entries; only a same-owner socket may be removed while holding the exclusive store sidecar. Bound command JSON to 4096 bytes and response JSON to 64 KiB, with a 5-second input timeout and 120-second command response timeout. The trusted same-UID operator can directly edit source/database; malicious same-UID edits are outside the threat model, but ordinary source races are caught by a candidate content digest over source/native/worker files in the request and verified by the server. Numeric release id is the only source selector; never a caller path. Commands hold a separate private command flock while allocating/staging immutable release source. They acquire the store owner when stopped; E_STORE_BUSY with a running serve uses the local socket instead of opening another writer. Dev and an older/unreachable server continue to refuse with an exact stop/retry command.

snapback4 releases <dir> reads authoritative current release, activation revision, generation and durable events without adopting source, including while serve is live. This is the recovery command for an ambiguous result. A bounded command message identifies the owned database inode/canonical identity, candidate release id, expected current release and monotone activation revision, operation and explicit flags. The server compiles only its own immutable release directory, prepares native module/worker prerequisites before commit, then holds its existing engine writer guard for activation and in-memory publication. The server verifies both expected current release and activation revision inside the activation transaction. Increment activation revision on every successful release activation, including an equal-hash source change; A→B→A cannot satisfy an old command. Concurrent commands serialize on the command flock; out-of-band stale commands fail compare-and-swap. No request may choose arbitrary filesystem source. Compilation/validation failure leaves the previous engine, bound schema, native module, worker revision and reader factory unchanged.

A query already running in a pinned SQLite read transaction finishes on its old snapshot and reports that generation. A writer already executing finishes before activation obtains its engine guard. Queued HTTP operations are admitted under one release request guard covering signature lookup, argument decoding and execution. Activation acquires its exclusive side before taking the engine guard; admitted operations finish before activation, and later operations dispatch under the new generation. Internal detached readers still fence obsolete identities with E_GENERATION. Fresh requests after publication use the new engine; a stale pooled reader not yet pinned retries through the replacement factory. Prepare a runtime bundle containing bound programs, precompiled native module/metadata, worker root and validated named declarations; prepare the reader factory from the post-activation engine while its writer guard blocks new writers (reader opening failure after commit clears the old factory and uses existing lazy retry, never aborts a committed activation or advertises the old backend). Swap bound/generation/native under the common bound guard in the existing /schema guard order, retire pooled readers, rebind ephemeral state, invalidate change answers, wake sync/polls and update worker revision while holding the writer guard. The supervisor explicitly switches its immutable release project root and named declarations on revision, including addition/removal/replacement; externally supplied --worker stays an operator-owned entry. Worker preparation captures an immutable (root, entry, declarations/environment, supervisor revision) tuple. Under the engine guard it compares that captured revision to the current worker revision before minting credentials; stale preparation is discarded and rediscovered. Never relabel an old prepared source with the latest revision. Credentials bind that checked revision so later activation fences them even when spawn finishes later. Workers restart using the candidate packaged source and native continuation hashes keep the existing conservative parking policy. Long validation can pause writes; hot means no process restart, not an unbounded zero-pause latency promise.

The server continues an admitted activation even if the client hits its 120-second response timeout or closes the socket; failure to write the reply is not permission to roll back or exit the server. The database is authoritative at crash or lost socket reply. Timeout/EOF after sending a command reports an ambiguous result and instructs inspection of current and durable history; never silently resend as a new release or claim failure left the old release active. Before activation fsync required immutable source/native/worker files and their directories, then the published releases directory entry. The supported guarantee covers process crashes and filesystem/SQLite durability on hosts honoring fsync; no guarantee covers failing storage hardware. Restart attaches the database-selected release even if current's convenience copy is stale. After database commit, publication is infallible in-memory assignments and committed ephemeral rebind; fallible native compilation, declaration/environment/import validation happens before commit. Worker process spawn remains fallible after commit: failure reports committed activation with degraded worker availability and retries; it cannot undo a release or external effects. Filesystem-copy failures report the committed result.

### 5.1 Path-independent control address

The accept loop polls a nonblocking listener, but immediately resets each
accepted stream to blocking mode before peer validation or input. Darwin/BSD
inherits the listener's nonblocking flag; Linux does not. Blocking streams
let the existing total input deadline govern clients that write after accept.
If resetting the stream fails, close it and report the accept error; never
admit a command on a stream with unknown I/O mode.

Amendment commissioned and implemented by Codex for Charlie on 2026-10-12,
for the 0.4.24 macOS publication blocker. Darwin's Unix socket path limit
is 104 bytes; the previous deployment-local name failed under ordinary
`/var/folders/.../T/` fixtures. Linux's descriptor-address workaround did not
solve Darwin or provide one portable transport.

Use `/tmp/snapback4-<effective-uid>/<sha256>.sock` on both hosts. The hash is
SHA-256 over the raw bytes of the canonical **deployment directory**, binding
the socket to immutable release source. Aliases that canonicalize to the same path resolve
to one address; two deployments pointing to one external database retain
separate addresses, so only the serving deployment can submit its releases.
At deploy and serve startup, create a missing deployment privately, resolve
its canonical directory once, then use that directory for ownership and all
release paths. Serve also resolves the selected release root before constructing
the project. Worker cwd and `SNAPBACK_PROJECT_DIR` therefore report the same
canonical active release path, including Darwin's `/var` → `/private/var` and
explicit deployment symlinks. An existing deployment alias resolves before
private-directory validation; no symlink is installed inside private storage.
Use the same canonical path spelling on case/normalization-insensitive hosts;
byte-distinct canonical spellings produce distinct addresses. The request
envelope still binds the canonical database path, device and inode.
The longest address is 95 bytes (ten decimal UID digits), below both limits.
Do not use TMPDIR or XDG_RUNTIME_DIR: these may be long or differ between CLI
invocations. This deterministic address needs no durable locator file; deploy
and rollback derive exactly the same address from durable deployment location.
A moved deployment must stop and restart serve, as moving its immutable source
already requires. Socket entries are ephemeral, never backup authority.

Create the single runtime directory atomically as 0700. Open with O_DIRECTORY
and O_NOFOLLOW, inspect the opened metadata, and refuse a different UID, a
non-directory/symlink or any mode other than 0700 (including special bits).
Never repair or chmod an existing directory. `/tmp` is the platform's shared
sticky temporary root (Darwin may alias it to `/private/tmp`). An untrusted
user may pre-create a conflicting name and cause refusal, but gains no access
and causes no modification of their entry. Malicious same-UID filesystem edits
remain outside §5's threat model.

Bind only while holding the exclusive database sidecar. An existing entry
must be a same-owner 0600 socket. Probe it: a live listener refuses without
unlinking; only connection-refused/not-found permits stale replacement. Any
other error refuses. Preserve the inode check around client connect, same-UID
peer checks on both ends, envelope identity, command flock and revision CAS.
Establish inode-based cleanup custody immediately after bind, before chmod or
nonblocking setup; Drop removes only that owned socket inode. Graceful shutdown
   460	pub(crate) fn producing_engine(shared:&Shared) -> engine_lock::FairGuard<'_,Engine> {

   466	/// A worker's seat: a bearer session of a service principal.

   468	    let engine=lock(&shared.engine);
   469	    let result=service_of_locked(request,shared,&engine);
   470	    if result.is_err() {crate::release_requests::stamp(engine.generation());}
   471	    result

   474	fn service_of_locked(request: &Request, shared: &Shared, engine: &Engine) -> Result<String, Refusal> {
   475	    dev_request(request, shared)?;
   476	    let refused = || Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "this is a worker's door: send a live service token (`snapback4 token --service <name>`)").guide("effects");
   477	    if !matches!(single_header(request, "x-snapback-persona"), Ok(None)) { return Err(refused()); }
   478	    let token = crate::auth::bearer_token(request).map_err(|e| e.refusal())?;
   479	    snapback4_core::identity::current_service(engine.store(), &token, snapback4_core::identity::ServiceSample {
   480	        now: shared.now(), supervisor_revision: shared.worker_revision.load(Ordering::Acquire),
   481	    }).map_err(|e| e.refusal())?.ok_or_else(refused)
   482	}

   484	pub(crate) fn single_header<'a>(request: &'a Request, name: &str) -> Result<Option<&'a str>, ()> {
   485	    let mut headers = request.headers().iter().filter(|h| h.field.as_str().as_str().eq_ignore_ascii_case(name));
   486	    let value = headers.next().map(|h| h.value.as_str());
   487	    if headers.next().is_some() { Err(()) } else { Ok(value) }
   488	}
   489	
   490	#[derive(serde::Deserialize)]
   585	    authority_host(authority)?;
   586	    Some(authority)
   587	}
   588	
   589	/// Browser developers use different local app ports. Trust literal loopback
   590	/// names, never DNS resolution: a foreign hostname may resolve to loopback.
   591	fn dev_request(request: &Request, shared: &Shared) -> Result<(), Refusal> {
   592	    if !shared.personas { return Ok(()); }
   593	    let host = single_header(request, "host");
   594	    let origin = single_header(request, "origin");
   595	    if !request.remote_addr().is_some_and(|addr| addr.ip().is_loopback())
   596	        || !request.url().starts_with('/') || request.url().starts_with("//")
   597	        || !matches!(host, Ok(Some(host)) if local_authority(host))
   598	        || !matches!(origin, Ok(None) | Ok(Some(_)))
   599	        || origin.ok().flatten().is_some_and(|origin| !origin_authority(origin).is_some_and(local_authority)) {
   600	        return Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "development HTTP requires a loopback Host and a loopback browser Origin; use localhost or a literal loopback address").guide("personas"));
   601	    }
   602	    Ok(())
   603	}
   604	
   605	// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.2 — public routes bypass dev admission, never the privacy boundary.
   606	pub(crate) fn trusted_dev_request(request: &Request, shared: &Shared) -> bool {
   607	    shared.personas && dev_request(request, shared).is_ok()
   608	}
   609	
   610	pub(crate) fn cors(request: &Request) -> Vec<Header> {
   611	    let mut headers = vec![
   612	        Header::from_bytes("Vary", "Origin").unwrap(),
   613	        Header::from_bytes("Access-Control-Expose-Headers", "X-Snapback-Time, X-Snapback-Clock, X-Snapback-Generation").unwrap(),
   614	        Header::from_bytes("Access-Control-Allow-Headers", "content-type, x-snapback-persona, x-snapback-meter, authorization, range, x-snapback-device").unwrap(),
   615	        Header::from_bytes("Access-Control-Allow-Methods", "GET, POST, OPTIONS").unwrap(),
   616	    ];
   617	    if let Ok(Some(origin)) = single_header(request, "origin") && origin_authority(origin).is_some() {
   618	        headers.push(Header::from_bytes("Access-Control-Allow-Origin", origin).unwrap());
   619	    }
   620	    if latency::headers_enabled() {
   621	        headers.push(Header::from_bytes("Access-Control-Expose-Headers", "Server-Timing").unwrap());
   622	    }
   623	    headers
   624	}

// Builds its own dist before starting proof servers.
// Replace the application assertions when replacing the starter screen.
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { command, startDev, startStatic, withBrowser, renderedText, fillRN, statusText, waitForFresh, bounded, cleanup, portRange } from "snapback4/proof";

// For SVG/foreignObject controls, click the actual rendered box, not a text
// descendant whose DOM geometry Playwright may interpret differently.
async function clickSvg(page, control) {
  await control.waitFor({ state: "visible" });
  const box = await control.boundingBox();
  assert.ok(box && box.width > 0 && box.height > 0, "SVG control has no rendered box");
  await page.mouse.click(box.x + box.width / 2, box.y + box.height / 2);
}

const options = process.env.SNAPBACK4_PROOF_LOGS ? { logsDir: process.env.SNAPBACK4_PROOF_LOGS } : {};
const range = portRange();
if (range !== "0" && range.split("-")[0] === range.split("-")[1]) throw Error("the starter needs two runtime ports");

      await fillRN(page.getByLabel("Password", { exact: true }), "Proof-password-928!");

      await page.getByRole("button", { name: personas[0], exact: true }).click();
    }

    26	    pub fn install(&mut self, schema: &Schema) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {

    28	    }

    30	    /// A device can discard an obsolete partition while atomically caching
    31	    /// its replacement backend and reset watermark. It never migrates rows
    32	    /// whose authority belongs to a previous server generation.
    33	    pub fn install_device(&mut self, schema: &Schema, metadata: &BTreeMap<String, String>, reset: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    34	        self.install_device_checked(schema, metadata, reset, &BTreeMap::new())
    35	    }
    36	
    37	    pub fn install_device_checked(&mut self, schema: &Schema, metadata: &BTreeMap<String, String>, reset: bool, expected: &BTreeMap<String, Option<String>>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    38	        self.install_device_checked_with(schema, metadata, reset, expected, |_| Ok(()))
    39	    }
    40	
    41	    /// Device-owned sidecar changes share the schema installation transaction.
    42	    pub fn install_device_checked_with(&mut self, schema: &Schema, metadata: &BTreeMap<String, String>, reset: bool, expected: &BTreeMap<String, Option<String>>, prepare: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    43	        self.install_device_checked_with_finish(schema, metadata, reset, expected, prepare, |_| {
    44	            Ok(())
    45	        })
    46	    }
    47	
    48	    /// Opening recovery and its final reads must finish before installation commits.
    49	    pub fn install_device_checked_with_finish<R>(&mut self, schema: &Schema, metadata: &BTreeMap<String, String>, reset: bool, expected: &BTreeMap<String, Option<String>>, prepare: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>, finish: impl FnOnce(&Tx<'_>) -> Result<R, RuntimeError>,
    50	    ) -> Result<R, RuntimeError> {
    51	        let mut result = None;
    52	        self.activate(schema, None, reset, |tx, _| {
    53	            for (key, value) in expected {
    54	                let held: Option<String> = tx.conn().query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = ?1", [key], |row| {
    55	                        row.get(0)
    56	                    }).optional()?;
    57	                if &held != value { return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("the backend adoption capture is obsolete; retry sync".into())); }
    58	            }
    59	            prepare(tx)?;
    60	            for (key, value) in metadata {
    61	                // A device metadata namespace reset shares backend installation's transaction.
    62	                if key.ends_with(':') {
    63	                    tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key >= ?1 AND key < ?2", params![key, format!("{key}\u{10ffff}")])?;
    64	                } else { tx.set_meta(key, value)?; }
    65	            }
    66	            if reset { tx.conn().execute_batch("DELETE FROM _device_base; DELETE FROM _device_snapshot;")?; }
    67	            result = Some(finish(tx)?);
    68	            Ok(())
    69	        })?;
    70	        Ok(result.expect("activation ran opening recovery"))
    71	    }

   571	    /// Replace the schema and programs in place (a successor generation);
   572	    /// the store keeps every row and rebuilds changed indexes; the
   573	    /// generation advances so every device re-derives its partition.
   574	    pub fn adopt(&mut self, schema: Schema, programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
   575	        self.adopt_at(schema, programs, crate::now_ms())
   576	    }
   577	
   578	    pub fn adopt_at(&mut self, mut schema: Schema, programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>, now: i64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
   579	        columns::validate_before_prepare(&schema)?;
   580	        schema.check_row_view_audiences()?;
   581	        schema.prepare_contribution_indexes();
   582	        schema.prepare_delivery_lookup();
   583	        let issues = crate::schema::validate(&schema);
   584	        if let Some(issue) = issues.first() {
   585	            return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}: {}", issue.path, issue.message)));
   586	        }
   587	        let programs = programs.into_iter().map(|p| (p.name.clone(), p)).collect();
   588	        drop(self.ephemeral.lock()?);
   589	        let (generation, metadata) = Self::activate(&mut self.store, &schema, &programs, None, now, true, None, None)?;
   590	        self.timeline_schedule = timeline::Schedule::default();
   591	        self.schema = schema;
   592	        self.programs = programs;
   593	        self.generation = generation;
   594	        self.expiry_after.clear();
   595	        self.ephemeral.rebind_committed(metadata.store_id, generation, &self.schema);
   596	        Ok(generation)
   597	    }

   368	    // Closing an accepted backend may checkpoint normally. Failure to restore
   369	    // this optional close-time optimization leaves the conservative setting;
   370	    // it must not turn a committed acceptance into a returned refusal.
   371	    pub(crate) fn accept_open(&self) {
   372	        let _ = self.conn.set_db_config(rusqlite::config::DbConfig::SQLITE_DBCONFIG_NO_CKPT_ON_CLOSE, false);
   373	    }
   374	
   375	    pub fn transaction(&mut self) -> Result<Tx<'_>, RuntimeError> {
   376	        self.check_ownership()?;
   377	        // The write lock and identity check are one boundary: an engine
   378	        // opened before another process activated a successor cannot write
   379	        // or read with its stale schema/catalog.
   380	        let tx = self.conn.transaction_with_behavior(rusqlite::TransactionBehavior::Immediate)?;
   381	        if activation::identity(&tx)? != self.identity {
   382	            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("the store adopted another backend; reopen this engine".into()));
   383	        }
   384	        Ok(Tx { activation_scan: None, changes: Default::default(), sequence_table: Default::default(), tx, page_size: self.page_size,
   385	            server: self.server,
   386	            catalog: &self.catalog })
   387	    }
   388	
   389	    /// A consistent read snapshot does not reserve SQLite's writer lock.
   390	    pub fn read_transaction(&mut self) -> Result<Tx<'_>, RuntimeError> {
   391	        self.check_ownership()?;
   392	        let tx = self.conn.transaction_with_behavior(rusqlite::TransactionBehavior::Deferred)?;
   393	        if activation::identity(&tx)? != self.identity {
   394	            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("the store adopted another backend; reopen this engine".into()));
   395	        }
   396	        Ok(Tx { activation_scan: None, changes: Default::default(), sequence_table: Default::default(), tx, page_size: self.page_size,
   397	            server: self.server,
   398	            catalog: &self.catalog })
   399	    }
   400	
   401	    /// One deferred snapshot for an authority walk from an API that only
   402	    /// borrows the engine. Writer operations pass their existing `Tx` instead.
   403	    pub(crate) fn authority_transaction(&self) -> Result<Tx<'_>, RuntimeError> {
   404	        self.check_ownership()?;
   405	        let tx = self.conn.unchecked_transaction()?;
   406	        if activation::identity(&tx)? != self.identity {
   407	            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("the store adopted another backend; reopen this engine".into()));
   408	        }
   409	        Ok(Tx { activation_scan: None, changes: Default::default(), sequence_table: Default::default(), tx, page_size: self.page_size,
   410	            server: self.server,
   411	            catalog: &self.catalog })
   412	    }
   413	
   414	    /// An independent read connection, bound to this catalog and identity.
   415	    pub fn reader(&self) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
   416	        self.check_ownership()?;
   417	        let path = self.conn.path().filter(|path| !path.is_empty())
   418	            .ok_or_else(|| {
   419	                RuntimeError::Store("an in-memory store has no independent reader".into())
   420	            })?;
   421	        let conn = Connection::open_with_flags(path, rusqlite::OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_READ_ONLY)?;
   422	        // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — detached readers must answer from the owned database, not a replacement at its path.
   423	        // Compare after opening, before reading. Stat only: closing another raw
   424	        // database descriptor would discard this process's SQLite POSIX locks.
   425	        #[cfg(unix)]
   426	        if file_identity(&conn).ok().flatten() != self.file_identity {
   427	            return Err(RuntimeError::Store(format!("owned database at {path} moved or was replaced; stop and reopen its current path")));
   428	        }
   429	        // This connection skips prepare's schema writes but needs the same
   430	        // planning policy before any statements are cached.

READY — source review of `90b029573b3e6a53ec6b789f73983c05c682c117` against `sb4-loop`.

No material correctness or security findings remain in the inspected implementation and published contracts.

- Staging classification belongs to the store. Existing production/legacy stores refuse relabelling; release switching preserves classification.
- Account seeding requires staging `serve`, uses real password signup, and provisions through an ordinary operator-authenticated mutation. The first-admin recipe independently restricts roster writes to `service:operator`.
- Password-only dev closes fixture, clock and persona doors by default. Privileged writes revalidate under the writer; persona data reads revalidate against their execution engine and declared cast. The protected local token protocol remains separate.
- Operator-token stdout uses secret capture. Credential records are returned directly, and the published recipe exclusively creates a private credentials file.
- Persistent `startDev` is reflected in the executable amendment: generated frontend proofs and published fixture/browser recipes explicitly request disposable storage. Older managed templates migrate by exact digest; custom scripts remain untouched.

The committed tests and packed qualification helpers exercise the principal promised boundaries. Their successful execution, including Linux packed and Darwin qualification, remains separate evidence—not established by this review.

No tests or Cargo commands were run. No files were edited; review artifacts and `WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md` contents were not read.
